My scrubs have been getting looser, and I’m not trying to lose weight. I just don’t feel like eating, and before a shift I might only manage coffee and a granola bar. My scrubs are hanging looser, and I’m not trying to lose weight.\n\nDoctor: How much weight would you say you've lost since you first noticed your eating habits change?\n\nPatient: I’m not exactly sure in pounds. I haven’t really been weighing myself. I just know my scrubs are fitting a lot looser, and it started after my eating got so off—some days it’s just coffee and maybe a granola bar before work.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in enjoyment of activities you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I used to run in Van Cortlandt Park, take photos, play chess with friends… stuff like that. Some days I barely manage coffee and maybe a granola bar before work, and my scrubs are starting to hang loose on me. I’m not trying to lose weight, so it’s been worrying me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost weight without trying to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. My scrubs have been getting looser, and I’m not trying to lose weight. I just haven’t been eating much—some days it’s just coffee and maybe a granola bar before my shift.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I’m not worried about gaining weight. It’s more that I just don’t feel like eating much lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that food doesn't taste as good to you as it used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s the taste exactly. It’s more like I just don’t feel like eating. I’ll get to work and realize I’ve only had coffee and maybe a granola bar, and lately my scrubs are fitting looser, even though I’m not trying to lose weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no interest in eating at all?\n\nPatient: Not no interest at all, but it’s pretty close some days. I just don’t feel like eating, and before a shift I might only manage coffee and a granola bar. My scrubs are hanging looser, and I’m not trying to lose weight.\n\nDoctor: How much weight would you say you've lost since you first noticed your eating habits change?\n\nPatient: I’m not exactly sure in pounds. I haven’t really been weighing myself. I just know my scrubs are fitting a lot looser, and it started after my eating got so off—some days it’s just coffee and maybe a granola bar before work.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in enjoyment of activities you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I used to run in Van Cortlandt Park, take photos, play chess with friends… stuff like that. Lately I just tell myself there’s no point, and I end up sitting at home scrolling on my phone or checking my budget spreadsheet instead.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, kind of hollow feeling, like the day’s already ruined before I even get going or put on my badge.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost a significant amount of weight, like more than 5% of your body weight, since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know the exact percentage. I haven’t really weighed myself. But yeah, it feels like a lot—my scrubs are hanging looser, and I’m not trying to lose weight. Some days I’m barely getting down coffee and a granola bar before a shift.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uncomfortable when you think about eating or when you're actually eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I’m not really scared of eating or uncomfortable with the food itself. I just… don’t have much appetite, and eating feels like effort sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Is your lack of interest in eating similar to your lack of interest in other activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s pretty similar. I used to run in Van Cortlandt Park, take photos, play chess with friends… stuff I actually liked. Eating kind of feels like that too—just hard to care enough to do it.\n\nDoctor: Has your weight loss been so significant that others have noticed or commented on it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a couple people have noticed. Someone at work mentioned my scrubs looked kind of baggy, and I can feel it too. I’m not trying to lose weight—it’s just that my eating’s been off, like some days it’s just coffee and maybe a granola bar before work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel like eating more if you weren't feeling so sad or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like the day’s already ruined before I even get my badge on. When I’m in that place, eating just feels like one more thing I don’t have it in me to do.\n\nDoctor: Did you start feeling sad and depressed before your eating habits changed, or did it happen after?\n\nPatient: The sadness came first, I think. The eating changed after that. The steady depressed feeling and not caring about things really settled in later, and it’s been going on for about… a year and four months now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you don't care about the things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: It’s been about a year and four months, I’d say. I’m not sure exactly when it started, but lately some days I barely get down coffee and a granola bar before work. It’s just gotten bad enough lately that before work I might only manage coffee and a granola bar, and my scrubs are fitting loose now even though I’m not trying to lose weight.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you don't want to eat?\n\nPatient: I don’t have an exact start date. It’s just gotten to the point where some days before a shift I barely manage coffee and maybe a granola bar, and my scrubs are hanging loose now even though I’m not trying to lose weight.\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling like you don't want to eat?\n\nPatient: I’d say it’s been a few months. I just don’t feel like eating, and before a shift I might only manage coffee and a granola bar.
